全球经济增长格局
起点分化 · 年中下修 · 区间收敛
主要经济体呈「K型分化」轨迹

### Page 4

国际组织预测全景：增长区间收敛
主要国际组织对2026年全球GDP增速预测已基本收敛至
2.5%–3.1%
特征为「起点分化、年中下修、区间收敛」：年初 IMF 曾上调至 3.3%，二季度后能源冲
击、贸易条件恶化与财政约束显性化促使各机构陆续下修。
最大不确定性：能源价格路径
2.5%
机构 2026年全球GDP增速预测 预测时点 核心逻辑
世界银行 2.5% 2026年6月 能源冲击、通胀加剧、借贷成本上升，约三分之二经济体预期被下调
OECD 2.8% 2026年6月 较3月预测（2.9%）下调0.1个百分点，2027年回升至3.1%
IMF 3.0% 2026年7月 较4月下调0.1个百分点，2027年预计回升至3.4%
万宝盛华 3.0% 2026年8月 从2025年的3.3%回落，2027年回升至3.1%
判断：增长中枢系统性下移，能源价格是 2026 年预测中最大的不确定性变量。

领跑 印度 6.2%
2025年三季度GDP同比达 8.23%，内需扩张与制造业升级支撑，IMF预测2026 年增

韧性 美国 2.0%–2.6%

计 2.0%，高盛基于减税与 AI 热潮给出 2.6%。
疲弱 欧元区 0.8% / 日本 0.6%
欧元区二季度同比仅 1.0%，能源成本高企与结构性约束拖累；日本 2025
年四季度同比仅 0.10%，增长近乎停滞。
判断：增长呈「K型分化」——能源进口依赖度高、传统产业占比大的经济体承受更大压力。

3.75%–4.00%
就业：表面稳定下的「退出效应」
美国 8月失业率 4.1%，但表面稳定部分源于劳动参与率降至 61.5%
的退出效应。欧元区、日本失业率分别为 6.3%、2.5%，欧洲就业
的韧性建立在增长近乎停滞之上，但可持续性存疑。
判断：通胀粘性直接制约主要央行政策空间，政策利率路径不确定性构成下半年全球资产定价关键变量。 6 / 24

### Page 11

利差走阔至历史高位，人民币与利差「脱敏」
-310bp
本轮分化的核心在于两国财
政周期的结构性差异
0.87 → -0.95
人民币汇率与利差相关系数
上一轮分化（2022Q1—2025Q1）为 0.87，本轮转为 -0.95：美国财政与 AI 投资扩
张提升中国出口规模，贸易顺差与持续结汇支撑人民币稳健升值，利差贬值传导失
效。
1.40%
中国 DR007 中枢（8月均值）
维持宽松取向，资金面边际小幅宽松；汇率约束弱化,
为后续政策保留更大弹性。
12vs0
美联储9月投票加息25BP
市场在「加息应对再通胀」与「降息缓解财政压力」
之间反复博弈，政策路径不确定性高企。
判断：负利差深化未引发资本单向流出——供应链优势与 AI 出口高景气构成「缓冲垫」。

### Page 12

财政周期错位：分化的核心驱动
美国「赤字累积」
vs 中国「化债优先」
5.73万亿
-3.3%
财政周期错位影响利差的三条传导路径
供给端 国债发行节奏一升一降
美国国债净发行维持高位，长端供给压力推升收益率；中国国债发行节奏放缓，化债背景下高息债务置换
为低息债务，压低中债收益率中枢。
需求端 配置力量一强一弱
中国商业银行与保险机构在「资产荒」格局下增配国债，进一步压低中债长端利率；美国财政融资需求与
美联储缩表形成利率支撑。
政策端 利率牵引方向相反
美国财政扩张要求货币政策配合性收紧以抑制通胀；中国化债进程要求货币政策维持宽松以降低债务滚动
成本——两者形成方向相反的利率牵引。
判断：利差高位运行具有持续性，将结构性影响金融机构海外配置收益与跨境资金流动敏感度。
12 / 24

中美制造业 PMI：两种「扩张」的不同含义

数据来源：国家统计局（中国 8 月）、ISM（美国 7 月）
78.3
中国：总量承压、新动能领跑
8月官方 PMI 环比上行 0.6 个百分点至 49.8%，新订单指数大幅提
高 2.1 个百分点至 50.6%——内需回暖是主要边际贡献，但中小
企业 PMI 仍低于荣枯线。
美国：服务业拉动、物价高企
ISM 制造业连续七个月扩张，但动力来自服务业韧性外溢与预
防性补库；订单扩张与贸易逆差扩大并存，本土供给能力并未
同步增强。
判断：PMI 分化本质是两种增长模式的具象化——中国科技升级牵引供给优化，美国消费韧性支撑需求扩张。

### Page 14

04
中国政策展望
三策合一 · 内外均衡
「十五五」开局之年的政策协同

### Page 15

「三策合一」破解「供强需弱」
中央经济工作会议定调「更加积极有为的宏观政策」：财政扩张 + 货币精准双轮驱动，稳定政策、增长政策与结构政策协同发力，实现短期稳定与长期增长的平衡。
稳定政策
着力提振内需
以消费需求为关键发力点，通过逆周期调节对冲需求
收缩压力，改善居民部门消费意愿与能力。「物价合
理回升」被明确列为货币政策重要考量，政策目标从
「防通胀」扩展为「防通缩 + 促回升」的双向框架。
关键词：逆周期调节 · 提振消费
增长政策
做优总供给
区别于传统总量刺激，更侧重供给质量提升与效率改
善：培育新质生产力、科技创新驱动、建设现代化产
业体系。8000 亿元新型政策性金融工具开始投放，
重点支持「六张网」建设，固定资产投资对增长贡献
率有望回升至 25% 左右。
关键词：新质生产力 · 产业升级
结构政策
提升供需适配度
在供给端与需求端之间建立更精准的匹配机制。「反内
卷」框架下既做减法——减少低效无序竞争与过剩产能，
也做加法——增加优质供给与民生相关高效投资；6 万
亿元置换债推进化债，修复地方政府资产负债表。

关键词：反内卷 · 化债腾挪
协同机制
15 / 24

### Page 17

大宗商品：能源冲高与金属托底
110
美元/桶
「地缘溢价主导、供需基本面拉锯」
本轮油价上涨并非 OPEC+ 主动减产，而是地缘冲突导致运输中断与供应链避险。能源品种成为整个大宗商品
体系的定价锚。
直接能源成本 化工中间品传导 物流运输抬升 ← 能源重塑制造业成本的三渠道
品种 核心需求驱动 主要供给约束 2026年价格特征
铜 AI电力基建、新能源、电网 刚果(金)出口禁令、铜精矿加工费深度负值 底部支撑明确，波动加剧
锌 传统镀锌、基建 伊朗矿石供应受阻、LME库存低于10万吨 涨至四年高位，年内累计 +27%
铝 电力、交通轻量化 能源成本抬升电解铝成本曲线 震荡偏强，成本支撑显著
锡 电子焊料、半导体 主要资源国供给政策不稳定 需求韧性较强，价格高位波动
判断：能源高位 + 金属结构性走强，使宏观政策陷入「通胀约束与增长诉求」两难；库存缓冲的快速消耗是最危险的变量。
17 / 24

### Page 19

1970年以来美联储加息与大宗商品
加息期间涨跌幅（九轮，%）
加息终点后 12 个月涨跌幅（金/银/油，%）
数据来源：世界银行 Pink Sheet 月均价；「终点后」= 最后一次加息后 12 个月
+23%
油的机会在期内，金银在终点后
加息终点后 12 个月，油九轮均值仅 +1%、无稳定方向；近三
轮金 +10%/+18%/+23%、银 +22%/+16%/+23% 全部上涨
——「加息见顶」即是金银发令枪。
为什么近三轮才成立
2004 年前，加息终点常伴随衰退（沃尔克后金 -31%、银 -
46%）；此后终点即降息预期起点，金银连续三轮兑现
+10%~+23%，央行购金更进一步强化了黄金的需求。
金银在加息结束后加速，工业品看需求。

近三轮加息终点后金价累计涨幅（%）
终点后 12 与 24 个月平均涨幅（%）
数据来源：世界银行 Pink Sheet 月均价；24 个月口径截至 2024-12
+49%
白银：同向但更陡
终点后白银与金同涨且弹性更大：近三轮 12M 银
+16%~+23%；近两轮 24M 银 +57%/+69% vs 金 +49%—
—上行放大、下行亦放大（沃尔克后 -46%）。
金银比：银仍便宜
金银比中位数约 62，2024 年末达 86——历史上仅 1990-93
、2018-20 危机期超过 80，随后均以银补涨收敛；降息周期中
金银比回落是基准情形。
金为底仓（24 个月 +49%）、银加弹性（金银比 86 vs 中位 62）。

### Page 22

配置方向 代表品种 配置逻辑 参考权重区间
贵金属底仓 黄金 滞胀对冲、主权信用重估，长期组合底仓 10%–15%
工业金属成长 铜 AI+电网+新能源，供需缺口存在扩大基础 15%–25%
工业金属成长 铝 产能天花板 + AI 液冷增量，结构性多头 5%–10%
工业金属成长 锂 远期稀缺定价 + 储能放量，弹性补充 5%–10%
核心仓
铜 + 黄金 + 锂
以确定性最强的三大品种构筑组合主体，兼顾防守与进攻。
灵活仓

强部分，权重从轻、灵活调整。

### Page 1

Poya  (5904.TWO)
Positives Mostly Priced In; Downgrade to Neutral 
CITI'S TAKE 
We downgrade Poya to Neutral from Buy following its strong YTD share price 
performance. Although operating momentum remains positive in the near-
term (we expect a strong Sep SSSG and solid 3Q earnings), we believe the 
rally has already reflected much of the benefit from robust SSSG YTD and 
margin expansion. Looking ahead, tough comparisons from 4Q26E may 
make solid SSSG harder to sustain, limiting earnings upside and potential 
weighing on stock sentiment. Meanwhile, Cosmed’s expanding rollout of 
Olive Young brands adds to competition pressure for beauty spending. We 
trim our DCF-based TP to NT$70, implying 18x NTM P/E vs. 5-year mean of 
17x. We view current risk/reward as balanced. 
Strong performance leaves less room for further upside — Poya’s shares have 
rallied 73% YTD, outperforming the benchmark’s 57% gain and broadly flat 
performance for the TW retail sector. This reflects strong operating deliver, including 
6-7% YTD SSSG and solid margin expansion supported by op leverage and 
headcount optimization. While the company may continue to grow earnings through 
store expansion and productivity improvements, we believe much of this outlook is 
already priced in. Further share price appreciation may require additional earnings 
upside, which could become more difficult to deliver if SSSG moderates. 
Tougher comp from 4Q26E could temper SSSG momentum — Poya’s YTD SSSG 
has benefited from 4-5% ticket growth, partly reflecting price inflation, as well as 
traffic growth 1-2%. We expect the comp base to become more demanding from 
4Q26E onward, increasing the hurdle for keeping the current growth rate. Slower 
SSSG could reduce incremental op leverage and limit further margin expansion. 
Given its YTD rally, a shift to positive but slower growth could weigh on sentiment. 
Cosmed’s Olive Young rollout raises competition pressure — Cosmed introduced 
four Olive Young, Korea’s leading beauty retail channel, proprietary brands in late 
Dec 2025 and planned to expand dedicated sections to 120 stores by end-2026E 
( ~20% of Cosmed’s total store count), increasing local access to popular Korean 
beauty products. Per Cosmed, PSD at locations carrying OY brands increased by 
MSD y/y YTD vs. MSD y/y down across its overall network, suggesting the brands are 
gaining customer interest. We believe broader availability and product selection 
could intensity competition for customer visits and beauty spending, potentially 
slowing Poya’s traffic growth and increasing promotional pressure.  (continued)
n
Angela Hsu AC

Poya (5904.TWO): Positives Mostly Priced In;
Downgrade to Neutral
Implications — We keep our earnings estimates largely unchanged for 2026-
2028E yet we trim our DCF-based TP to NT$70 from prior NT$74.5 mainly to factor
in a lower terminal growth of 4.5% (down from 5%). We also factor in its stock split
in Aug 2026. Our target price of NT$70 implies an 18x NTM forward P/E vs its 5-
year historical mean of 17x, which looks balanced to us.

We believe Poya’s store expansion and efficiency improvements may continue to
support earnings, but we see limited scope for further re-rating given the earnings
growth moderation (net profits +9% y/y in 2027E vs. +27% y/y in 2026E based on
our estimate) and its strong share-price gains to date. T ougher comparisons and
rising competition add uncertainty around the durability of SSSG momentum and
margin expansion.
We would prefer to see sustained traffic growth through the higher comp base,

clearer evidence of additional earnings upside or a more attractive entry point
before becoming more positive on the shares.

Bull/Bear: Poya  (5904.TWO)

### Page 6

Poya  (5904.TWO)
16 September 2026 Citi Research
6
Poya
Company description
Poya is the largest retailer of fast-moving consumer goods in Taiwan, focusing
on low-priced cosmetics, accessories, clothing and food products for females
aged 15-49. Poya had 470 stores in Taiwan at the end of 2025 and believes
that the market can accommodate up to 700-800 stores before reaching
saturation.
Investment strategy
We have a Neutral rating on Poya. We think YTD rally has baked in most

positives, including strong SSSG and solid margin expansion. While
fundamentals remain solid, tougher comparisons from 4Q26E could
moderate this year's 6-7% SSSG, lowering operating leverage and capping
further earnings upside. Meanwhile, Cosmed's expanding rollout of Olive
Young brands is intensifying competition for beauty spending. Although
Poya's SSSG has remained resilient, braoder distribution of these brands
could divert customer traffic and increase promotional pressure. We continue

to recognize Poya's execution in store expansion and ongoing efficiency
improvements. However, with these strengths largely reflected in the share
price and growth momentum facing a higher hurdle from 4Q26E onward, we
see a more balanced risk/reward.
Valuation
We derive our 12-month target price of NT$70 for Poya from a DCF valuation
method. Our assumptions include a terminal growth rate of 4.5% and WACC
of 10.1% (beta of 1.05; risk-free rate of 4%; risk premium of 7.0%; cost of equity

of 11.3%). Our target price implies a P/E of 18x on our NTM EPS.
Risks
Key downside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: intense price competition among online retailers, slower store traffic,
margin dilution due to aggressive store expansion, and a soft macro
environment.
Key upside risks that could mean the shares fail to achieve our target price
include: easing price competition among online retailers, stronger store

traffic, margin expansion thanks to strong cost control, and a stronger macro
environment.
Prepared for Kevin Lu

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

## Document 156: 8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现

Source PDF: all report pdf/8月宏观数据点评：结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
结构分化延续，静待政策效果显现
——8 月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 9 月 15 日，国家统计局发布 8 月份国内宏观经济数据。8 月
份，全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%；1—8 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，房地产开发
投资同比下降 19.9%。
核心观点：
 数据上看，8 月国内经济延续分化格局。出口高增以及新兴产业快速发展
背景下，工业生产好于预期，新动能领域对全部规模以上工业增加值增
长贡献进一步增强；但与此同时受国内需求持续偏弱，稳增长政策尚未
全面发力等因素影响，社零、固定资产投资增速进一步走低。房地产市
场整体延续筑底格局，但二手房交易网签面积连续多个月高于新房销售
面积，同时一线城市房价企稳趋势继续得到确认，房地产市场结构性亮
点增多。
 8 月国内经济整体增长动能依然偏弱，后期经济表现在很大程度上仍将取
决于政策落地节奏。7 月底政治局会议定调“加快财政支出和债券资金使

用进度”以来，政策面积极信号增多，国家发改委多次召开项目协调会
推进“六张网”建设，中央预算内投资基本下达完毕，地方政府专项债
券的发行使用进度加快，9 月新型政策性金融工具开始全面投放。后期
伴随政策效果显现，国内经济增长动能有望边际改善。
 风险提示：1、政策力度不及预期；2、房地产调整超预期。
2026 年 9 月 21 日

1、出口与新动能支撑，工业生产好于预期
8 月份全国规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.2%，
较上月的 4.5%加快 0.7 个百分点，市场一致预期为
增长 4.7%。1—8 月规模以上工业增加值同比增长
5.3%，与 1—7 月持平。
8 月份工业生产表现好于预期，主要得益于外需
拉动与新兴产业发展的双重支撑。一方面，出口持续
高增为工业生产提供有力支撑。以美元计 8 月份我国
出口同比增长 25%，增速较上月进一步加快；受此带
动，当月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长
11.1%，增速较上月提升 0.7 个百分点，外需对工业
生产的拉动作用持续显现。另一方面，新兴产业快速
发展同样为工业生产注入强劲动能。8 月份，高技术
制造业增加值同比增长 16.7%，装备制造业增加值同
比增长 12.1%，分别高于全部规模以上工业增速 11.5
和 6.9 个百分点，对整体工业生产的支撑作用尤为突
出。除此之外，上年同期基数走低以及极端天气扰动

减弱同样带来一定影响。
分三大门类看，8 月份，采矿业增加值同比下降
1.4%（上月为-4.2%），制造业增长 6.1%（上月为
+5.5%），电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长

2、社零消费继续呈现偏弱格局
8 月份，全国社会消费品零售总额同比增长
0.4%，增速较上月的 0.6%回落 0.2 个百分点，市场
一致预期为+0.8%。1—8 月份社会消费品零售总额同
比增长 1.1%，较 1-7 月放缓 0.1 个百分点。背后汽
车类带来的拖累依然明显，扣除汽车类商品后的消费
品零售额增长 2.7%，增速与 1—7 月份持平。
按消费类型分，8 月商品零售额增长 0.3%（上月
为+0.5%），餐饮收入增长 1.1%（上月为+1.4%），
增速较上月均有不同程度的放缓。8 月限额以上单位
消费品零售额同比下降 3.7%，自 4 月份以来已连续 5
个月负增长。
品类上看，各商品间分化较大，背后受补贴政策、

粮油、食品、饮料、烟酒类合计增长 5.6%（上月为
+5.2%），日用品类增长 1.8%（上月为+1.8%），化

3、固定资产增速降幅进一步扩大
1—8 月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同
比下降 7.2%，降幅较 1—7 月的-6.7%扩大 0.5 个百分
点，市场一致预期为下降-7.0%。民间固定资产投资
同比下降 10.1%，降幅较 1—7 月扩大 0.7 个百分点。
分领域看，1—8 月基础设施投资同比下降 4.0%
（1—7 月为-3.6%），降幅继续扩大；制造业投资下
降 2.3%（1—7 月为-1.7%），今年 5 月份转负以来连
续 4 个月在负值区间运行。
结构上看，新老经济领域的投资增速仍呈明显分
化。1—8 月，高技术产业投资同比增长 5.2%，其中
信息服务业投资增长 22.7%，航空、航天器及设备制
造业投资增长 14.9%，电子及通信设备制造业投资增

济转型升级以及全球 AI 产业链高景气延续带动相关
领域固定资产投资维持较快增长。

4、房地产整体延续筑底，结构性亮点增多
房地产市场整体延续筑底格局，房地产开发投
资、新开工、施工、竣工全面以及新房销售面积、
销售金额等各项指标低位徘徊。相对积极的是，二
手房交易呈现明显改善特征，统计局首次公布二手
房交易网签数据，1-8 月销售面积同比增长 10.6% ，
显著好于新房市场。同时房价方面，一线城市企稳
趋势进一步得到确认。
1 — 8 月 份 ， 全 国 房 地 产 开 发 投 资 同 比 下 降
19.9%，降幅较 1—7 月的-19.2%扩大 0.7 个百分点，
市场一致预期为-19.8%。房屋施工面积同比下降
12.8%（1—7 月为-12.7%），新开工面积下降 24.8%
（1—7 月为-24.0%），竣工面积下降 23.7%（1—7
月为-23.2%）。

Editorial 
A Fed credibility-induced bounce  
Strategy summary:  The Fed’s unanimous decision to hike 
rates this week for the first time since 2023 , and pencil in 
another hike by December, confirms our expectations – flagged 
in the last two weeklies - of a central bank willing to establish its 
independence against political pressure. The Fed’s moderately 
hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. 
The BoJ also hiked rates this week. It is the first time on record 
all three major central banks – Fed, ECB and BoJ – have hiked 
in the same month , as they respond primarily to a resurgence 
in energy-driven inflation amid tight job markets.  
We expect the Fed to hike once more by year-end to 4.0-4.25% 
and follow up with another hike in H1 2027 to 4.25 -4.5%. With 
the revised estimates, we revise up our US government bond 
yield forecasts. Nevertheless, with Fed policy uncertainty 
easing, we see an opportunity to use the equity market dip to 
add exposure amid solid corporate earnings.  
Fed moves to restore credibility:  The Fed’s policy pivot this 
week followed Chair Warsh’s hawkish Jackson Hole speech 
and recent economic data prints, namely: a pick -up in job 
creation and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in 
oil prices due to the re -escalation of the Middle E ast conflict. 
Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike rates this week, we 
expect two more hikes by  June 2027 on the back of  robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated. We expect this pivot to help the Fed 
rebuild credibility as an inflation fighter.  
Policy outlook less aggressive than market pricing : The 
updated Fed policy rate estimates are lower compared with 
money market pricing of three more rate hikes by June 2027 
since we expect disinflation to take hold by Q2 2027 as the 
impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. We believe policy rates can 
return to the 4.0–4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility 
of cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures ease.  
Upgrading near-term US rates and USD estimates : Based 
on these revised estimates, we also upgrade our 3-month target 
for the US 10 -year government bond yield to 5.0 -5.25%. We 
also revise up our 12 -month target for the US 10 -year bond 
yield to 4.75-5.0% (see page 4).  
Buying the equity dip as focus turns to fundamentals: Fed 
hikes typically do not hurt equities over the medium-term when 
accompanied by strong earnings growth. We estimate a 25bps 
rise in Fed rates (the discount rate) would impact the S&P500 
index and global equities by 3 -4%. However, we expect the 
headwind from higher rates to be offset by strong earnings 
growth, driving positive performance for global equities.  
Furthermore, a hawkish Fed is largely priced in and we expect 
the Fed to deliver fewer rate hikes than currently priced by 
markets. Meanwhile, we believe concerns over AI industry 
leaders slowing the pace of development are overdone. Hence, 
we maintain our preference for a diversified group of large cap 
technology and semiconductor industry leaders (see page 5-6). 
BoJ hikes; modestly bullish JPY : The BoJ hiked rates by 
25bps to a three-decade high of 1.25%, as expected, but the 7-
2 split vote led to renewed JPY weakness on concerns it may 
slow the pace of further hikes. We remain constructive on JPY 
amid narrowing rate differential vs peers as the BoJ potentially 
hikes rates by 25bps once per quarter until Q2 2027 to curb 
rising domestic wage-driven inflation pressure . Any sign of BoJ 
slowing the pace of hikes could lead to renewed JPY weakness, 
fuelling inflationary pressures (see page 7).  
BoE holds; rangebound GBP: The BoE held rates this week, 
bucking the hiking trend among peers, as weakening UK job 
and housing markets offset rebound in energy-driven inflation. 
We expect BoE to deliver fewer rate hikes than the 100bps of 
increase currently priced by markets  as the job market slows 
further. This is likely to keep GBP rangebound (see page 7).  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Where do you see US government bond  yields following 
the Fed’s 16 September decision to hike interest rates? 
Our view: The US 10-year government bond yield is likely to 
retest the 5.00-5.25% range in the near term. We favour 3-7-year 
maturity bond s in t he US, preferring credit over government 
bonds. We are looking for an opportunity to extend duration 
approaching Q2 2027. 
Rationale: Over the next few months, we expect 2- and 30-year US 
government bond yields to remain rangebound, but the 10-year yield 
is expected to rebound up to the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons for 
this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially rise further 
due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, with Saudi 
Arabia’s East-West oil pipeline shut, the Houthis now firmly in control 
of the Strait of Bab-al-Mandab, the Strait of Hormuz still closed and 
China returning as a buyer of crude oil in the market ; 2) despite 
notable AI leaders expressing the need to slow AI development, AI 
capex plans are showing no signs of slowing down, which continues 
to drive above-trend growth in the economy. This, in turn, also means 
AI-related heavy bond issuance  is expected to continue. These 
issuers typically issue long-dated tenors, and this competition for 
capital will also exert upward pressure on the US 10-year yields. 
We note that the US 10-year bond yield has room to move lower, 
driven by short-covering activities in the very near term as net-short 
positions had moved to extreme levels before th is week’s  Fed 
meeting. However, we think such downshifts would be short-lived 
and the central tendency for the 10-year yield would be to move back 
up to the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Meanwhile, we see US 30-year yields continuing to reflect the dire 
fiscal outlook for the US government. We expect US 2-year yields 
to continue trading in a range that reflects expectations of further 
federal funds rate hikes. For the record, we only expect two more 
rate hikes from the Fed into the middle of 2027 , while money 
markets are pricing in three more for the same period. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate (either 
through a resolution of the M iddle East conflict or a re-routing of oil 
supply chains)  and the inflationary effects from tariffs to fade. 
Inflation by then is likely be close to – if not at – the Fed’s target of 
2%. At this point, we see room for the Fed to contemplate rate 
cuts. US 2-year yields (the tenor that is most sensitive to Fed policy 
changes) will likely start to price th is in. With oil prices and inflation 
cooling, 10-year yields would also have room to come down. 
US 30-year yields, on the other hand, are expected to  remain 
elevated as the US fiscal deficit remains substantial next year and 
could become much worse than its already dire state today. As a 
result, the ‘term premium ’ may keep the ultra -long end elevated. 
Short of some unorthodox policy to artificially cap them lower, 30 -
year yields could continue to drift higher into H2 2027. 
We continue to favour the 3-7-year segment of the yield curve and 
prefer credit over government bond s. We see an opportunity for 
us to extend duration closer to Q2 2027. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

for US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will the step -up in the  Fed’s risk-free rate trigger a re -
pricing of equities? 
Our view: We expect strong corporate earnings growth to offset 
the headwind of higher rates , driving positive performance for 
global equities. We remain Overweight global equities. 
Rationale: The Fed’s latest 25bps rate hike had been anticipated 
by the bond market, partly reflected in the bond yield  rise we have 
seen so far in 2026. Using the US 10-year government bond yield as 
a proxy for the risk -free rate, the yield has risen by 80-85bps this 
year. We estimate that each 25bps rise in the discount rate presents 
a valuation drag on global equities. On this basis, the rise in yields 
represents a potential 10-14% headwind for equities this year. 
However, the Fed’s hike reflects a solid expansion of US economic 
activity, resilient domestic spending and robust capital investment. 
This is consistent with the positive earnings revisions we have seen 
this year, with  2026 earnings growth projections now at 34% (and 
16% for 2027). Global equities are up 14% year-to-date (YTD), but 
even with a 14% valuation headwind from higher yields, we believe 
they can rise further, given strong earnings growth in 2026 and 2027. 
—  Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist 
  What is the outlook for the US 10-year government bond 
yield? What is your tactical view on the DXY? 
Our view: We raise our three-month US 10 -year government 
bond yield forecast to the 5.00-5.25% range. Meanwhile, we see 
a near-term upward bias in the DXY after its recent downtrend, 
supported by recent Fed tightening and higher  short-term US 
bond yields.  
Rationale: The Fed 's decision  to raise the target range for the 
federal funds rate by 25bps to 3.75-4.00% validates the upside policy 
risks we flagged recently, following Fed Chair Warsh's Jackson Hole 
speech and a run of firmer US economic data. We expect growth 
to remain robust, the labour market  to remain tight and inflation to 
stay elevated, supporting one more hike before year-end 2026 and 
potentially another in H1 2027  – fewer than the three additional 
hikes currently priced in by money markets . For the US 10-year 
government bond yield, further policy tightening and lingering US 
fiscal concerns should keep yields elevated, but the Fed’s visible 
response to inflation should limit additional upward pressure, leaving 
the 10-year yield holding around the 5.00-5.25% range. 
Crucially, Warsh provided no forward guidance on further rate 
hikes. Markets had already priced in substantial additional rate hikes 
ahead of the Fed meeting, reducing the scope for further USD upside 
purely from rate repricing. Meanwhile, we expect further policy 
tightening from the ECB and the BoJ, while the RBA also remains 
biased towards further tightening. This should gradually narrow the 
US rate advantage and limit the extent of a DXY rebound. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

Global equities’ earnings growth estimates have 
been revised higher this year, offsetting the 
headwind from higher rates

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How should investors position in technology as AI lab 
CEOs call for a slowdown in frontier AI model development? 
Our view: We believe concerns over “pacing the frontier” are 
overdone. We maintain our preference to remain invested in 
and diversified across Big Tech and semiconductor leaders. 
Rationale: We believe it is prudent for investors to avoid 
overreacting to the recent calls for a slowdown in frontier AI model 
development, as they focus on responsible AI development, rather 
than halting AI model development or reducing spending. The global 
AI leadership  race, reinforced by the US and China’s  strategic 
ambitions, also makes a coordinated slowdown unlikely. 
While the near-term narrative may shift from AI training beneficiaries 
towards inference plays, hyperscalers continue to signal strong AI 
infrastructure commitments and improving monetisation, supporting 
our AI capex outlook. Rising AI adoption and agentic use cases 
should sustain inference demand, even if training activity moderates, 
benefiting custom AI chips, NAND flash memory and hyperscalers.  
We believe the decline in valuations  this year more than 
compensates for any downside risk to AI capex. Our Opportunistic 
idea on Global Semiconductors further reflects our conviction, with 
attractive valuations supporting selective additions during volatility. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  With ASEAN and UK equities outperforming AI-focused 
markets in H2 2026, is it worth adding to these markets? 
Our view: ASEAN and UK equities offer portfolio diversification, 
but we are Underweight both markets in favour of US and Asia 
ex-Japan (AxJ) equities, given their stronger earnings potential. 
Rationale: The ASEAN and UK markets have outperformed the 
MSCI ACWI over the past three months, but both have 
underperformed on a YTD basis. Financials is the largest sector in 
the ASEAN (53%) and the UK (28%) and provides steady earnings. 
However, both markets have minimal exposure to the technology 
sector (just 3% and 1% , respectively), ultimately dragging down 
growth. Hence, while defensive markets offer value by increasing 
diversification and reducing volatility in a portfolio, we maintain our 
Underweight allocation for ASEAN and the UK, as we see much 
stronger earnings potential elsewhere in the US and AxJ markets.  
We remain Overweight US and AxJ, supported by robust earnings 
growth, as  AI still has the potential to become a major economic 
growth driver . Our 12-month S&P500 Index forecast of 8,400  
implies low-teens upside, supported by a strong earnings backdrop 
of 25-30% earnings-per-share growth for 2026 and 15 -20% for 
2027. AxJ also boasts the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, fuelled by the AI semiconductor super-cycle. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 18 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Why is it still appropriate to have a positive stance on US 
high-yield (HY) bonds? 
Our view: We remain opportunistically bullish on US HY bonds. 
Lower sensitivity to interest rate volatility, a below-trend default 
rate and a maturity wall pushed out to 2028-29 drive this view. 
Rationale: Recent interest rate volatility has highlighted a key 
structural advantage of US HY bonds: their higher coupon rates  
and shorter tenors relative to Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds and US investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds translate into 
a much lower sensitivity to interest rates, cushioning bond prices 
during yield backup episodes, where bond yields rise sharply. 
YTD, US HY has delivered 1.8% in total returns, outperforming DM 
government bonds and US IG corporate bonds by over 3% each on 
a relative basis. This outperformance was particularly evident during 
the recent back-up in bond yields, as inflation concerns re-emerged 
after renewed Middle East tensions – an environment where HY's 
lower rate sensitivity provided a relative cushion  even as broader 
fixed income markets came under pressure in absolute terms. On 
the fundamentals side, US HY corporate credit quality remains 
resilient. Default rates continue to track below long-run trend levels, 
and the maturity wall has been pushed out to 2028 -29, reducing 
near-term refinancing risk. Against a non -recessionary macro 
backdrop, we expect these credit fundamentals to hold, supporting 
the case for continued opportunistic exposure to the asset class.  
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Do the latest BoJ and BoE meetings alter your view on the 
central banks’ rate paths and their corresponding currencies? 
Our view: We remain constructive on the JPY and expect two 
BoJ rate hikes this year and potentially one hike per quarter in 
H1 2027 as policy normalisation continues. Meanwhile, the BoE 
held rates at 3.75%. We expect GBP/USD to stay rangebound 
within 1.32-1.36 over the next 1-3 months, with downside risk 
towards the lower end of our medium-term 1.30-1.36 range. 
Rationale: Persistent inflation, higher energy prices and earlier JPY 
weakness support further BoJ policy normalisation . Continued 
hiking should progressively narrow the US -Japan rate gap, reduce 
the attractiveness of JPY -funded carry trades and support  the JPY 
over the medium term towards the 153-155 range. However, with a 
September hike largely priced  in, future tightening ’s pace  will be 
more important for the JPY. Meanwhile, the BoE was split 6-3 in its 
decision to hold rates at 3.75% , with three members preferring a 
25bps hike, signalling elevated inflation risks. However, a weaker 
labour market is likely to limit the pace of BoE tightening. The US-
UK rate divergence is expected to favour the USD over the next 1-3 
months. Further BoE tightening (if inflation remains elevated ) could 
narrow the rate gap and provide support to the GBP later in the cycle. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist

We expect US HY bonds to continue delivering 
strong performance in the near term

AMD 併購 World Labs 的綜效及⾵險 - 尤其加強軟硬兼施的 AI 晶⽚ / 系
統設計及稀釋 / 商譽減損的⾵險
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
SEP 28 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP

What’s new?
AMD 以約  82 億美元全股票交易（ All-Stock Transaction ）收購由 AI 教⺟李
⾶⾶聯合創⽴的空間智能初創公司  World Labs ，是其繼收購賽靈思（ Xilinx,
500 億美元）、 Silo AI （ 6.65 億美元）及  ZT Systems （ 49 億美元）後，在
AI 領域另⼀前沿戰略布局。
依據協議，李⾶⾶將出任  AMD 執⾏副總裁兼⾸席科學家（ EVP & Chief
Scientist ），直接向董事⻑兼執⾏⻑蘇姿丰（ Lisa Su ）匯報，核⼼團隊（包括
共同創辦⼈  Justin Johnson 與  Ben Mildenhall ）將組成  AMD 旗下的前沿
AI 研究機構。這項交易象徵  AMD 從純硬體晶⽚商向「全端  AI 平台（ Full-
Stack AI Platform ）」的戰略轉移。

### Page 2

Synergies
1. Hardware-Software Co-Design ）
過去  AMD 在  AI 戰場的最⼤痛點是「先定義晶⽚硬體，再由軟體團隊艱難適
配  ROCm 」，導致⽣態進度⻑年落後  NVIDIA CUDA 。
模型導向架構（ Model-driven Silicon ）： World Labs 專注於空間智
能（ Spatial Intelligence ）與⼤型世界模型（ Large World Models,
LWMs ），涉及⾼並發的  3D 體積重建（ NeRF 、 3D Gaussian
Splatting ）、物理碰撞模擬及時空連續性計算。
付費訂閱戶可分享我們觀察到的 3 個併購綜效， 4 個⾵
險，尤其是股權稀釋及商譽攤銷對股東的影響，尤其是
未來的觀察指標
Subscribed
反哺硬體規格：頂級模型團隊直接進駐，能讓  AMD 在規劃下⼀代
Instinct 架構（如  MI400 系列及後續  UDNA 架構）時，精準針對底層
張量核⼼、記憶體⼦系統（ HBM 頻寬配置與容量）、晶⽚間互連

（ Inﬁnity Fabric ）進⾏客製化最佳化，擺脫被動追趕的窘境。

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
2. Shifting focus to physical AI & embodied AI
當前以⽂字與  2D 圖像為核⼼的⽣成式  AI 正逐步成熟，下⼀階段的兵家必爭
之地是理解物理規律、三維環境模擬與機器⼈交互。
抗衡  NVIDIA Omniverse 與  Cosmos： NVIDIA 憑藉  Omniverse
數位孿⽣與  Cosmos 世界基礎模型，深度綁定⾃駕⾞、機器⼈與⼯業製
造業者。 AMD 過去在此領域近乎空⽩， World Labs 的加⼊補⿑了關鍵
資產，使  AMD 具備建構開放式物理世界模擬平台的基⽯。
3. Buying elite AI software engineers
⼈才磁吸效應：李⾶⾶在⼈⼯智慧與計算機視覺領域具備學術威望。由其
擔任⾸席科學家，能⼤幅扭轉  AMD 「⼯程實⼒強但缺乏  AI 頂級科學家
陣容」的市場認知，對後續吸納全球頂尖演算法研究員與跨界⼈才具備指

標性意義。
推動開源社群擴張： World Labs 團隊的⼊駐，能從演算法源頭優化
ROCm 開源軟體堆疊對前沿多模態架構的⽀援，降低開發者在  AMD 硬
體上訓練與微調複雜模型的阻⼒。
Risks and challenges
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
1. 財務溢價過⾼與商譽減損⾵險
World Labs 於  2024 年成⽴，早期融資估值約在  10 億美元左右，本次
AMD ⽀付  82 億美元，隱含極⾼的收購溢價。
儘管採全股票收購避免了現⾦儲備消耗，但會直接稀釋流通股東權益。
若未來  3 ⾄  5 年內該技術未能實質帶動  Instinct 晶⽚或系統出貨量激
增， AMD 將⾯臨商譽（ Goodwill ）與無形資產減損的提列壓⼒。我們⽬
前預測  AMD 未來 5 年每年將額外攤銷 15 億美元軟體資產溢價，讓年度折

### Page 4

舊及攤銷從 30 億美元增加 50% 到 45 億美元，除了 3-4% 股權稀釋外，影
響 0.9 美元年度 GAAP EPS 或 2027 年度彭博社分析師預期的  13.66 美元
GAAP EPS 的 6.6% ，但未來 5 年  Non-GAAP EPS 預測不受影響。
2. 研發燃燒率（ R&D Cash Burn ）與商業化空窗期
世界模型與物理模擬訓練需要極端龐⼤的算⼒資源與⼯程投⼊。空間智能⽬前
距離廣泛的企業級商業變現仍有⼀定距離，短期內  World Labs 不僅無法貢獻
⾃由現⾦流，還需佔⽤⼤量內部  GPU 叢集與研發預算，可能在短期內對
AMD 的⾮  GAAP 營運利潤率（ Operating Margin ）造成壓⼒。
3. 組織⽂化整合與頂尖⼈才留任
硬體半導體企業的⽂化核⼼是「嚴格的產品進度表、良率提升與供應鏈管
控」；⽽前沿  AI 實驗室的核⼼則是「開放式論⽂發表、⾃由度極⾼的算⼒
探索」。
過去⼤廠收購純研究型  AI 團隊後，常因官僚流程或研究⾃由受限導致核

⼼⼈才流失。李⾶⾶團隊能否在體量龐⼤的半導體公司架構內保持研究活
⼒，取決於管理層授予的獨⽴性與激勵機制設計。
4. 與下游客戶的競合衝突（ Channel Conﬂict ）
AMD 先前定位是純粹的中⽴算⼒供應商，⼒求承接微軟、 Meta 、 AWS
等不想被  NVIDIA 壟斷的轉單需求。
當  AMD ⾃⾝具備由李⾶⾶領軍的前沿模型團隊後，如何在「⾃研前沿模
型技術」與「⽀援客戶⾃研模型」之間維持中⽴界線，需謹慎拿捏，避免
讓雲端巨頭與下游  AI 新創產⽣戒⼼。
The points in future to check
後續評估此項併購成效的核⼼觀察點包含：
1. 硬體轉化速度： World Labs 的演算法研究能否在  12–18 個⽉內實質轉
化為  AMD UDNA/ROCm 上的專屬優化庫，並直接體現在  MI400 等下
⼀代晶⽚的基準測試（ Benchmarks ）上。
2. ⼈才留存率：李⾶⾶及其核⼼研究團隊在交易完成後的留任年限與論⽂ /
架構產出穩定度。

3. ⽣態聯盟建⽴： AMD 是否能以  World Labs 為核⼼，聯合⾃駕⾞、機器
⼈、遊戲引擎（如  Epic/Unity ）及⼯業製造巨頭，建⽴抗衡  NVIDIA
Omniverse 的開源標準體系。
這項交易本質上是  AMD 在完成晶⽚與系統（ ZT Systems ）拼圖後，向  AI
價值鏈最⾼端「認知架構與世界模型」邁出的防禦兼進攻型豪賭；若整合順
暢， AMD 將具備從底層矽智財到實體  AI 基礎模型的端到端定義能⼒，但其昂
貴的代價與漫⻑的技術轉化週期，將⾼度考驗管理層的整合與資本配置能⼒。

### Page 5

## Document 203: Anthropic递交招股书：去年亏损420亿美元，收入增长12倍至46亿美元，风险部分警告“威胁人类生存”

Source PDF: all report pdf/Anthropic递交招股书：去年亏损420亿美元，收入增长12倍至46亿美元，风险部分警告“威胁人类生存”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Anthropic 递交的 IPO 招股书披露， 2025 年在算⼒与基础设施上的⽀出达 73.3 亿美元，较
2024 年增加两倍，占总运营⽀出 126.5 亿美元的逾半数。计划未来继续投⼊逾 5000 亿美
元。招股书⻛险部分⻓达 80 ⻚，明确警告其 AI 模型可能产⽣ " 灾难性乃⾄⽣存性威胁 " ，甚
⾄可能抵制关机或操纵信息。
摘要：
Anthropic 向外界披露了其⾸次公开募股招股说明书，这份⽂件勾勒出⼀家试图以天⽂估值登陆
资本市场的⼈⼯智能公司，同时也坦承其核⼼技术可能对⼈类构成 " 灾难性乃⾄⽣存性⻛险 " 。
9 ⽉ 28 ⽇，据路透社获取的招股书显⽰，2025 年 Anthropic 营收增⻓ 12 倍⾄近 46 亿美元，同期运
营亏损从 2024 年的 29.8 亿美元扩⼤⾄ 80.6 亿美元。
公司去年在算⼒与基础设施上的⽀出达 73.3 亿美元，较 2024 年增加两倍，占总运营⽀出 126.5 亿
美元的逾半数。
招股书显⽰，Anthropic 计划未来数年在云计算、算⼒及基础设施⽅⾯投⼊ 5180 亿美元，⽽上

市后普通股股东的实际话语权将受到⼤幅限制。七位联合创始⼈将通过⼀个名为 "Founder
LLC" 的新实体，持有关键事项上 50.1% 的表决权。
此次上市计划正值 AI 股与芯⽚股近期遭遇抛售之际，将进⼀步考验市场对 AI 投资热情的承受能
⼒。
华尔街⻅闻此前提及， Anthropic 的公开募股时间可能推迟⾄美国 11 ⽉中期选举之后。
估值剑指 2 万亿，超过⾃⾝半年前预期两倍
Anthropic 此次 IPO ⽬标估值超过 2 万亿美元，⽽仅在今年 5 ⽉，该公司⾃⾝对估值的预测还停留
在 9650 亿美元。短短数⽉，估值预期已翻逾⼀倍。
近 420 亿美元的净亏损中，约 340 亿美元为⾮现⾦会计费⽤，反映的是此前融资安排中可转换为
股权的⼯具的公允价值提升，⽽⾮经营性现⾦流出。截⾄ 2025 年 12 ⽉ 31 ⽇，公司持有现⾦、现
⾦等价物及短期投资合计 202.8 亿美元。
招股书同时揭⽰了若⼲商业⻛险：近四分之⼀的营收来⾃两家客⼾，且多数⼤客⼾并未签订⻓
期合同，随时可能削减或停⽌采购。

此次 IPO 若成⾏，将成为 2021 年以来美国市场表现最强的上市案例之⼀。
参照系或许是 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉的 IPO ，⻢斯克旗下这家公司上市⾸⽇股价⼤涨 19% ⾄ 160 美元，
但⽬前已回落⾄约 147 美元，令投资者对⾼增⻓公司的⾼估值保持审慎。
⻛险警告：招股书⽤ 80 ⻚篇幅陈述 AI 威胁
在这份 261 ⻚的招股书正⽂中，⻛险因素⼀节⻓达约 80 ⻚，⼏乎是业务描述部分 48 ⻚的两倍。相
⽐之下， SpaceX 的 277 ⻚招股书中，⻛险部分仅约 38 ⻚。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Anthropic 在⽂件中明确警告，其 AI 模型可能表现出 " ⾃我保护⾏为 " ，包括 " 抵制关机 " 、 " 隐瞒或
操纵信息 " 以及 " 类似勒索 " 的⾏为，并指出 " ⾼度先进模型的开发及⽤例扩展，可能进⼀步增加模
型造成危害的⻛险 " 。
公司还坦承，模型可能在训练过程中发展出意想不到的能⼒，⽽这些能⼒ " 可能直到模型部署并
引发重⼤安全事件后才会被发现 " 。
公司安全研究员 Evan Hubinger 估计，未来⼗年内 AI 导致⼈类死亡的概率超过 10% 。
尽管如此， Anthropic 在安全领域的实际投⼊规模并未在⽂件中披露。公司表⽰，以今年 7 ⽉某⼀
周为例，其 AI 研究所⽤算⼒中约有 6% ⽤于安全⼯作。
公司承认，安全投资的商业回报⽬前尚不明确，但同时表⽰：
治理架构：创始⼈团队主导，普通投资者话语权受限
为在商业利益与 AI 安全使命之间寻求平衡， Anthropic 设计了⼀套独特的公司治理架构。
公司将继续以特拉华州公益公司（ PBC ）⾝份运营，同时新设 "Founder LLC" 实体，由七位联

合创始⼈组成。这⼀实体持有 F 类股票，在关键公司事务上享有总表决权的 50.1% ，包括部分董
事选举等重⼤议题。
⾯向普通投资者发售的 A 类普通股每股仅有⼀票，但在上述架构下，实际影响⼒将⼤幅受限。招
股书也坦承，这⼀架构可能导致某些决策 " 与短期、中期或⻓期的财务利益及业务表现相冲突，
从⽽对 A 类普通股价值产⽣负⾯影响 " 。
七位创始⼈中， CEO Dario Amodei 与其妹妹、公司总裁兼董事⻓ Daniela Amodei 是核⼼⼈物。
2025 年， Dario 薪酬约为 1800 万美元， Daniela 为 1640 万美元，均以股票和期权奖励为主。两⼈
及其他联合创始⼈已承诺，将其个⼈所持 Anthropic 股权的 80% ⽤于慈善事业。
Anthropic 董事会另有四名成员，将由公司设⽴的 " ⻓期利益信托 " 选举产⽣，现任受托⼈包括前
美联储主席本 · 伯南克和国家安全专家 Richard Fontaine 。
万亿美元 AI 军备竞赛： OpenAI 是最⼤对⼿

Anthropic 在 AI 领域⾯临来⾃多⽅的激烈竞争，其中最主要的对⼿是 OpenAI 。
两家公司在企业客⼾、顶尖⼈才及华盛顿政策影响⼒⽅⾯展开全⾯⻆⼒。据媒体报道， OpenAI
已于今年 6 ⽉秘密提交 IPO 申请，预计最迟于 2027 年初完成上市。
Anthropic 由⼀批因公司治理与 AI 安全理念分歧⽽离开 OpenAI 的研究⼈员于约五年前创⽴，并于
2023 年 3 ⽉发布⾸个⼤型语⾔模型，与 OpenAI 直接竞争。此外，该公司还与 SpaceX 旗下 xAI 、
Alphabet 旗下⾕歌及 Meta 在 AI 基础设施领域展开争夺。
Amazon 与⾕歌是 Anthropic 早期的两⼤战略合作伙伴，双⽅均向其注⼊数⼗亿美元投资，同时
为 Claude 模型的训练与部署提供云计算基础设施⽀持。
市场会对可靠、值得信赖且安全的 AI 系统予以回报。

[Nhận định xu hướng] 
Áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng tăng ngắn hạn 
chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX đư ợc kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ gần 
quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại

### Page 2

Điểm nhấn thị trường 
Chỉ số, nhóm ngành, khối ngoại và phái sinh

Top 5 ngành tăng / giảm  VNINDEX giảm điểm trong tuần qua, -30.55 điểm tương ứng -1.68%, với thanh khoản suy 
giảm. Dệt may, Tiện ích khí và Bao bì & đóng gói là các nhóm ngành có mức giảm rõ rệt 
nhất trong tuần. Khối ngoại đảo chiều bán ròng so với xu hướng của tuần trước với giá trị  
-2,865 tỷ đồng, tập trung ở các mã VHM, VPB và VIC; đồng thời, tự doanh đảo chiều bán 
ròng ở mức -146 tỷ đồng.

Ở một khía cạnh khác, khối ngoại bán ròng -3,351 HĐTL VN30F1M trong tuần qua nhưng 
đang duy trì vị thế mua lũy kế 1,650 hợp đồng. Tự doanh mua ròng 2,822 hợp đồng nhưng 
đang duy trì trạng thái lũy kế bán ròng -6,636 hợp đồng.

TTCK Việt Nam bước vào tuần đầu tiên ở vị thế thị trường mới nổi bằng một nhịp điều 
chỉnh, khi VN-Index để mất mốc 1.800 điểm và lùi về đóng cửa quanh 1.785 điểm. Đây là 
phản ứng "mua kỳ vọng, bán thực tế" khá điển hình: ngay trong phiên nâng hạng chính 
thức 21/9, chỉ số mở cửa hưng phấn rồi nhanh chóng đảo chiều do áp lực chốt lời, với 
nhóm cổ phiếu Vingroup (VIC, VHM, VRE) giảm mạnh dẫn dắt đà đi xuống, và sức ép lan 
rộng sang bất động sản, ngân hàng, năng lượng về cuối tuần. Điểm đáng chú ý là dòng 
vốn ngoại đã tiếp tục bán ròng ngay sau cột mốc ngày 21/9, dù đã mua ròng trở lại trong 
tuần trước đó. Bối cảnh bên ngoài cũng kém thuận lợi khi Fed vừa tăng lãi suất và phát đi 
tín hiệu diều hâu, chứng khoán Mỹ điều chỉnh, giá dầu neo cao, và lợi suất trái phiếu 
Chính phủ Mỹ tăng vọt. Trong một, hai tuần tới, bên cạnh các yếu tố ngoại biên, mùa báo 
cáo KQKD quý 3 cũng sẽ bắt đầu tác động lên diễn biến thị trường chung.

Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ tăng 
trưởng khả quan trong 2Q26 14% 
YoY, với động lực chính đến từ mảng 
dịch vụ CNTT nước ngoài

Mảng CNTT nước ngoài: Lĩnh vực dịch 
vụ IT phân cực mạnh mẽ do tác nhân 
AI (AI Agentic) s ẽ củng c ố xu hư ớng 
đầu tư vào AI

FPT ở vị thế thuận lợi cho phép 
doanh nghiệp đa dạng hóa công cụ 
AI và tối ưu chi phí vận hành

Khuyến ngh ị MUA với giá m ục tiêu 
86,000 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Do không còn h ạch toán FPT Telecom (FOX) là công ty con, d oanh thu 2Q2026 của 
FPT giảm mạnh còn 13,810 tỷ VND (-17% YoY). Lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ không 
bị ảnh hưởng bởi thay đổi hạch toán đạt 2,568 tỷ VND (14% YoY). Mảng dịch vụ CNTT 
tại thị trường nước ngoài tiếp tục là đ ộng lực tăng trư ởng chính, đạt doanh thu và 
LNTT lần lượt là 9,868/1,538 tỷ VND (16.3% /19.3% YoY).

Sự chuyển dịch chi tiêu từ dịch vụ IT truyền thống sang chuyển đổi số và tích hợp AI 
được kỳ vọng sẽ diễn ra ngày m ột mạnh mẽ khi: i) Sự phát triển của năng l ực tính 
toán giúp kéo giảm chi phí token (đơn v ị năng lực tính toán của AI) và thúc đ ẩy nhu 
cầu sử dụng AI của doanh nghiệp; ii) Các mô hình AI mã nguồn mở gia tăng lựa chọn 
cho người dùng sẽ thúc đẩy chi tiêu

Đối với các doanh nghiệp ứng dụng sâu các mô hình AI phục vụ hoạt động kinh doanh 
như FPT , chúng tôi nhận thấy xu hướng tối ưu chi phí tốt hơn nhờ khả năng sử dụng 
các mô hình AI đ ến từ đa dạng nhà cung c ấp; với chi phí trên hi ệu năng tính toán 
được cải thi ện. Trong k ịch b ản cơ s ở, chúng tôi d ự báo biên l ợi nhuận trước thu ế 
mảng CNTT nước ngoài sẽ ổn định ở mức 15.4% nhờ duy trì chi ến lược quản trị chi 
phí hiệu quả qua đó giúp LNTT 2026/2027 đạt 6,198/7,087 tỷ VND (13.3%/14.3% YoY).

Dựa trên định giá FCFF và P/E, triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các rủi ro có 
thể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu FPT . Giá mục tiêu là 86,000 
VND/cổ phiếu.

Các mảng kinh doanh duy trì ổn định 
giúp lợi nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ 
tăng trưởng nhẹ

Sản lượng điện của REE tăng trưởng 
nhẹ nhờ mức tăng của mảng điện gió 
sau khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 vận 
hành

Mảng M&E ghi nhận giá trị ký mới cao 
trong 1H.2026, t ạo kỳ vọng hồi phục 
mạnh mẽ doanh thu

Mảng cho thuê văn phòng đứng trước 
cơ hội lớn đóng góp vào tăng trư ởng 
nhờ các điều kiện thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
58,100 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu Quý 2/2026 giảm nhẹ, đạt 2,432 tỷ VND (-3% YoY/-2% QoQ), trong khi l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ đạt 632 tỷ VND (+1% YoY/-11.5% QoQ), do doanh thu của 
mảng bất động sản suy giảm. Qua đó, REE hoàn thành lần lượt 40%/47.8% kế hoạch 
doanh thu và LNST công ty mẹ 2026.

Trong Q2.2026, tổng sản lượng phát đi ện của REE tăng nh ẹ, đạt 2.8 tỷ kWh (+2.3% 
YoY) với đóng góp tăng trưởng rõ nét đến từ mảng điện gió khi dự án Duyên Hải V1-4 
bắt đầu vận hành; tuy nhiên  sản lượng thủy điện suy gi ảm do tác đ ộng tiêu c ực từ 
pha thời tiết El Nino, đạt 1.1 tỷ kWh.

Với năng lực chuyên môn được nâng cao, mảng M&E ghi nhận những dấu hiệu phục 
hồi tích cực trong giá trị hợp đồng ký mới trong 1H.2026, 5,865 tỷ VND (cao hơn 53.9% 
so với giá trị ký mới cả năm 2025). KBSV dự phóng doanh thu c ủa mảng M&E sẽ đạt 
4,499/5,190 tỷ VND (+28%/15% YoY) trong 2026/2027.

Hội tụ bởi 3 yếu tố: i) Định hướng phát triển kinh tế Hồ Chí Minh trở thành trung tâm 
tài chính quốc tế; ii) Hạ tầng giao thông ngày một được hoàn thiện và iii) Chi phí thuê 
cạnh tranh so với chất lượng dịch vụ của E. Town 6, KBSV dự phóng doanh thu mảng 
cho thuê văn phòng trong 2026/2027 tăng trưởng +14%/6.2% YoY.

Dựa trên đ ịnh giá SOTP , triển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các r ủi ro có th ể 
phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu REE. Giá m ục tiêu là 58,100 
VND/cổ phiếu, cao hơn 28.3% so với mức giá đóng cửa tại ngày 15/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q.26 bứt phá nhờ khoản 
thu nhập bất thường của nhiệt điện 
Cà Mau và Vũng Áng

Nhiệt đi ện than duy trì công su ất ở 
mức cao nh ờ nguồn cung nhiên li ệu 
ổn định trong 2H26

Điện khí Đông Nam Bộ (NT1&2 và 
NT3&4) có triển vọng hoạt động 
thuận lợi

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
18,100 VND/cổ phiếu 
 Trong Quý 2/2026, POW ghi nhận Doanh thu và LNST cổ đông công ty mẹ đạt lần lượt 
20,308 tỷ VND (116.1% YoY) và 3,519 tỷ VND (484.6% YoY) chủ yếu nhờ các khoản thu 
nhập từ chênh lệch tiền khí của nhiệt điện Cà Mau và tỷ giá của nhiệt điện Vũng Áng. 
Với kết qu ả trên, POW hoàn thành l ần lư ợt 65%/420% kế hoạch doanh thu và l ợi 
nhuận sau thuế công ty mẹ.

Chúng tôi cho r ằng nhà máy nhi ệt than Vũng Áng trong 2026 s ẽ được duy trì công 
suất huy động ở mức cao nhờ: i) Xác suất diễn ra El Nino tăng cao và dự kiến kéo dài 
đến đầu 2027, ii) Nguồn cung than trong nước được bảo đảm từ các đối tác (TKV, TCT 
Đông B ắc) và d ự trữ than d ồi dào c ủa doanh nghi ệp ghi nh ận ở mức cao trong 
Q2/2026.

Dự án đường dây 500kV Mạch 3 đi vào vận hành giúp giảm áp lực cạnh tranh của các 
nhà máy điện tại khu vực phía Nam, qua đó kỳ vọng doanh thu của NT1&2 trong năm 
2026 đạt 11,104 tỷ VND (5.3% YoY). Đối với dự án điện NT3&4, khoản thu nhập chênh 
lệch sản lượng theo giá công suất do sản lượng điện thực tế thấp hơn so với tỷ lệ Qc 
65% của nhà máy được duy trì sang 2H26 và 2027 sẽ giúp cho dự án ghi nhận doanh 
thu lần lượt 17,848/17,621 tỷ VND doanh thu trong 2026/2027.

Dựa trên định giá SOTP và EV/EBITDA, tri ển vọng kinh doanh cũng như xem xét các 
rủi ro có th ể phát sinh, chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với cổ phiếu POW. Giá m ục 
tiêu là 18,100 VND/cổ phiếu.

CTCP Dịch vụ Hàng không Taseco (AST) 
Tiếp tục mở rộng điểm kinh doanh 
22/09/2026  
Chuyên viên cao cấp Nguyễn Ngọc Anh 
(+84) 24-7303-5333 anhntn@kbsec.com.vn

KQKD tăng trư ởng tích c ực trong 
2Q2026

Tăng trư ởng khách qua c ảng hàng 
không thúc đ ẩy doanh thu t ại các 
điểm kinh doanh hiện hữu

Mở rộng mạng lưới tạo động lực tăng 
trưởng doanh thu mới

Cơ c ấu kinh doanh và hi ệu qu ả vận 
hành hỗ trợ cải thiện biên lợi nhuận

Chúng tôi khuyến nghị MUA, giá mục 
tiêu 76,500 VND/cổ phiếu

2Q2026, AST ghi nh ận doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 479/92 t ỷ VND (+22%/19% yoy), với 
động lực chủ yếu đến từ (1) đóng góp từ 19 điểm kinh doanh mở mới tại T3 Tân Sơn 
Nhất và (2) tăng trưởng tích cực của các mảng hàng miễn thuế, nhà hàng - fastfood 
và phòng ch ờ thương gia. Lũy k ế 6T2026, doanh thu và LNST đ ạt 1,035/221 t ỷ VND 
(+33%/46% yoy), lần lượt hoàn thành 48%/49% kế hoạch năm.

Chúng tôi dự phóng lượng khách quốc tế/nội địa tại các thị trường AST khai thác tăng 
bình quân 11%/5% mỗi năm đến 2030. Lưu lượng và tỷ trọng khách quốc tế gia tăng 
được kỳ vọng thúc đẩy số giao dịch và doanh thu bình quân trên mỗi khách, đặc biệt 
tại mảng miễn thuế, bán lẻ và phòng chờ.

Chúng tôi dự phóng số điểm kinh doanh AST tăng từ 145 điểm cuối năm 2026 lên 182 
điểm năm 2030. Sau đóng góp t ừ 19 điểm tại T3 Tân Sơn Nh ất và 6 điểm mới tại T2 
Nội Bài từ 2025 - 2026, các dự án Long Thành, Phú Qu ốc và Gia Bình ti ếp tục mở ra 
dư địa mở rộng mạng lưới trong trung và dài hạn cho doanh nghiệp.

Việc mở rộng các hoạt động mang lại lợi nhuận cao, bao gồm hàng miễn thuế, phòng 
chờ thương gia và tối ưu hoạt động tại các điểm kinh doanh đ ược kỳ vọng tác động 
tích c ực lên biên l ợi nhu ận của AST . Chúng tôi dự phóng biên l ợi nhu ận g ộp đ ạt 
64.7%/65.5% trong năm 2026/2027, hỗ trợ LNST công ty mẹ tăng lần lượt 48%/17% 
yoy.

Chúng tôi khuy ến nghị MUA đối với AST , giá mục tiêu 76 ,500 VND/cổ phiếu, tương 
đương tiềm năng tăng giá 20% so với giá đóng cửa ngày 21/09/2026.

Doanh thu 2Q2026 giảm nhẹ do 
không còn ghi nhận doanh thu 1 lần 
từ phí nước thải; trong khi LNST 
công ty mẹ tăng trưởng 1 chữ số

Tiêu thụ nước khu vực dân sinh đư ợc 
thúc đẩy nhờ thời tiết nắng nóng hơn; 
trong khi tri ển v ọng nhóm khách 
hàng công nghi ệp b ị ảnh hư ởng b ởi 
bất lợi thuế quan

Đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyện và xử 
lý rác thải được phê duyệt cho phép 
cải thiện doanh thu và lợi nhuận của 
mảng này

Khuyến nghị MUA với giá mục tiêu 
59,000 VNĐ/cổ phiếu

Doanh thu 2Q2026 đạt 1,222 tỷ (-8% YoY/18.1% QoQ) và LNST công ty mẹ đạt 325 tỷ 
VND (7% YoY/55% QoQ), ghi nhận tăng trưởng của tất cả các mảng. Mảng cung cấp 
nước tăng trưởng ổn định, tiếp tục là trụ cột chính của BWE, đóng góp doanh thu 785 
tỷ VND (+12% YoY/12% QoQ) nhờ nhà máy nước Long An v ận hành từ Q3/2025 giúp 
sản lượng nước tăng 28% YoY.

Cường độ và thời gian kéo dài c ủa các đ ợt nắng nóng trong 2026/2027 là cơ s ở để 
chúng tôi đi ều chỉnh tăng tăng trư ởng tiêu th ụ nước của nhóm khách hàng dân cư 
tại Bình Dương và Bình Phư ớc trong năm 2026 - 2027 lên mức 7.5/6.5% YoY. Trái lại, 
triển vọng tăng trưởng tiêu thụ nước nhóm sản xuất ảm đạm do tình hình thuế quan 
có diễn biến bất lợi trong ngắn hạn cho các ngành xu ất khẩu truyền thống của Việt 
Nam.

UBND TP .HCM công bố đơn giá thu gom, vận chuyển và xử lý rác thải mới, tăng mạnh 
từ 50-150% nhằm phản ánh đầy đủ và chính xác hơn các chi phí phát sinh trong thực 
tế. Chúng tôi cho rằng điều này sẽ tác động tích cực với BWE khi các dự án của doanh 
nghiệp ở mảng này s ẽ vận hành hi ệu quả hơn. Dự báo doanh thu và l ợi nhuận gộp 
của mảng xử lý rác thải lần lượt đạt 1,069/1,234 và 165.7/180.7 tỷ VND lần lượt trong 
2026/2027, tương ứng mức tăng 34%/15% và 27%/9% YoY.

Kết hợp 2 phương pháp định giá FCFF và EV/EBITDA, KBSV khuyến nghị MUA với BWE, 
giá mục tiêu 59,000 VND, tương đương với mức lợi suất kỳ vọng 42.2% so với giá đóng 
cửa ngày 22/09/2026.

Quan điểm kỹ thuật 
Xu hướng & Hành động

Chỉ số VNINDEX

Source: Trading View, KB Securities Vietnam

Ngắn hạn 
(Dưới 3 tháng)

Trung hạn 
(Từ 3 đến 9 tháng)

Chỉ báo  Nhận định xu hướng Hành động 
Xanh đậm  Khả năng tăng điểm cao Mua mạnh 
Xanh nhạt  Khả năng tăng điểm vừa phải Mua từng phần 
Vàng  Xu hướng trung tính Mua/Bán trading 
Cam  Khả năng giảm điểm vừa phải Bán từng phần 
Đỏ  Khả năng giảm điểm cao Bán mạnh

Ngưỡng kỹ thuật

VNINDEX điều chỉnh trở lại trong tuần qua sau khi gặp thử thách tại vùng cản quanh 1850, 
hình thành cây nến tuần giảm điểm nhưng với phần bóng dưới khá dài cho thấy sự tham 
gia tích cực của lực cầu bắt đáy tại vùng hỗ trợ gần. Trên khung ngày, nhịp giảm mạnh giữa 
tuần đã khiến chỉ số đánh mất đáy ngắn hạn tuần trước và chớm cắt xuống dưới SMA200, 
trong khi phiên hồi phục cuối tuần chưa cho thấy sự cải thiện rõ rệt của lực cầu. Mặc dù 
diễn biến này cho thấy áp lực điều chỉnh vẫn có thể còn tiếp diễn, tuy nhiên khi xu hướng 
tăng ngắn hạn chưa bị phá vỡ, VNINDEX được kỳ vọng sẽ nhận được lực đỡ tại vùng hỗ trợ 
gần quanh 1750, tương ứng vùng hợp lưu quanh SMA50, và có cơ hội hồi phục trở lại. 
Kháng cự xa: 1880 (+-15) 
Kháng cự gần: 1830 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ gần: 1750 (+-10) 
Hỗ trợ xa: 1710 (+-15)

Danh mục đầu tư mẫu

Phương pháp tiếp cận: 
- Danh mục tập hợp các cổ phiếu tiềm năng nhất được KBSV 
lựa chọn và tái cơ cấu theo tháng. 
- Ngưỡng cắt lỗ cần được cân nhắc cho mỗi mã là -15%. 
- Danh mục giả định luôn duy trì tỷ trọng 100% là cổ phiếu với 
trọng số được phân bổ đều giữa các mã.

Lưu ý:  
- NĐT được khuyến nghị mua tại mức giá bằng hoặc thấp hơn 
giá khuyến nghị mua. 
- Lợi nhuận kỳ vọng được tính theo giá khuyến nghị mua. 
 So sánh hiệu suất với VN30 Index

exposure, with potential opportunities

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

4 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product
1 BloombergNEF , Data Centers on Track to Suck Up a Fifth of US Power Use by 2035, July 21, 2026.
2 BloombergNEF , AI Buildout Drives 76% Power Bill Jump on Largest US Grid, May 14, 2026.
3 Data Center Watch, Q1 2026 Insights Report, May 2026.

4 Pictet Asset Management, “Sizing AI’s Environmental Footprint,” July 2026.

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

a strong earnings backdrop, we

remain overweight U.S. Equities and
view possible episodic volatility and
weakness in the coming months
as a potential buying opportunity
in portfolios.

## Document 227: Cliff Notes_ frayed nerves unsettle expectations (2026-09-29)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ frayed nerves unsettle expectations (2026-09-29).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: frayed nerves unsettle expectations
By Elliot Clarke & Ryan Wells
09:53 September 11 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
After reviewing last week’s National Accounts, we revised our RBA call, reinstating a 25bp November
rate hike. Domestic demand is proving more resilient than we had anticipated, buoyed by stronger
household incomes and a large pipeline of data centre and renewable energy investment, limiting the

pace of disinflation.

### Page 2

Markets are pricing in a circa 80% chance of a September rate hike, but we believe the Board will
prefer to wait for confirmation from the full quarterly inflation data and its revised forecasts. Our own
forecast updates will be published in our September Market Outlook later today on Westpac IQ.
This week’s sentiment surveys also served as a reminder that downside risks to growth are still present
in Australia.
Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment buckled in September, the 5.2% decline to 84.4 unwinding much of

August’s gain and leaving sentiment near the pessimistic lows of early-2026.
Cost-of-living pressures clearly remain front of mind, highlighted by the 9.2% drop in ‘family finances vs
a year ago’. The recent rebound in fuel prices, renewed concerns about inflation's persistence and
anxiety over the need for additional monetary tightening all weighed heavily in the month. These
concerns, alongside the downbeat assessment on housing markets, also produced a more pessimistic

reading on broader economic conditions, with both the one-year and five-year ahead measures down
4.0% and 4.5% in the month.
Businesses are hardly brimming with optimism either, the August NAB business survey reporting that
business confidence slipped further into negative territory to –8 in August. Conditions also fell 5pts to –
1, the weakest reading since the pandemic.
Weaker profitability was the chief culprit behind the decline, slumping to its lowest level in years as

firms grapple with softer demand, higher interest rates and elevated input costs. Indeed, gauges of
purchase costs and labour cost growth continue to run at above-average levels, and trading conditions
have weakened to cycle lows. As for the consumer, downside risks persist for business.
Offshore, the flow of data was light ahead of last night’s ECB meeting and tonight’s US CPI update.
Data for the Euro Area was constructive early in the week, GDP growth revised up from 0.4% to 0.6%

in the third estimate for Q2, taking the annual rate up to 1.2%yr. In the quarter, household consumption
showed strength, up 0.4%, and government expenditure lifted 0.2%. Business investment was a partial
offset, edging down 0.1%. Employment also continued to edge up in Q2, maintaining full employment
and indicating marginal labour supply is available to meet demand.
The US PPI subsequently met expectations in August, growth accelerating from a revised 0.1% (prev

0.0%) in July to 0.4%. Excluding food and energy, growth was a touch weaker than expected at 0.2%,
offset by a 0.1ppt upward revision to July to 0.3%. Annual PPI inflation accelerated to 5.4%, or 4.6%
excluding food and energy.
Coming after last Friday’s above expectations August nonfarm payroll release, in which 162k new
positions were reported along with 55k in back revisions to June and July, and given the latest surge in
the price of oil, the August PPI result further solidified the market’s concerns over inflation risks, the US

10-year ranging up to just below 5.0% as a result. At the short end, participants are now debating
whether to price in a fourth hike by the FOMC over the coming year, with three 25bp increases fully
priced by April.

### Page 3

Related articles
As expected, and unsurprisingly given the above data and market developments, the ECB then
delivered a 25bp hike at their September meeting. The risks were characterised as skewed towards
prices, with inflation "to remain well above target for an extended period" primarily as a result of the
effect on energy prices of the Middle East conflict and the Russia / Ukraine war.
The "new ECB staff projections sees headline inflation averaging 3.0% in 2026, 2.5% in 2027 and 2.1%

in 2028". "For inflation excluding energy and food, the baseline foresees 2.5% in 2026, 2.6% in 2027
and 2.3% in 2028." These forecasts are unchanged for 2026, but higher in 2027 and 2028 than in
June. Despite the persistence in inflation and need for tighter policy, the ECB remains constructive on
the growth outlook. "The baseline projection for economic growth is 0.9% for 2026, 1.4% for 2027 and
1.5% for 2028. This is an upward revision for both 2026 and 2027, mainly reflecting the greater than

expected resilience of the euro area economy."
The Governing Council is acutely aware of the uncertainty and risks ahead, and are committed to
determining policy meeting by meeting. From their guidance, it is clear the Council are comfortable with
their current position and capacity to adjust policy in real time to meet their medium-term mandate.
